# disk-cleanup

A Mac dev-machine cleanup playbook. Runs in two phases: **survey** (read-only,
report candidates with sizes) → **execute** (delete only what the user OKs,
category by category). Never delete in the survey phase. Never assume the
user wants the whole list — wait for explicit picks.

## Guiding principles

1. **Confirm before deleting.** Always survey first, present sized candidates
   grouped by category, and only act on what the user explicitly approves.
   Do not infer "yes to all" from a yes on one category.
2. **Rebuildable > irreplaceable.** Prioritize things that regenerate on next
   `npm install` / `cargo build` / `pip install` / app restart. Treat
   recordings, Downloads, and project source as user data — only touch on
   explicit instruction.
3. **Blast radius matters.** A deleted `node_modules` costs a reinstall. A
   deleted `.git` costs work. Show the path before deleting; never glob
```

name: megaplan
description: AI agent harness for coordinating Claude and GPT to make and execute extremely robust plans.
---

# Megaplan
Route every step through the `megaplan` CLI. Never call agents directly.
Before the first CLI call, resolve a working launcher and reuse it for the whole run. Do not assume `megaplan` itself is on `PATH`; command presence alone is not enough. Prove the launcher works by successfully running a harmless CLI call with it first. In the instructions below, treat `<launcher>` as that verified command.
Launcher resolution order:
1. Try `python -m megaplan config show`.
2. If that fails, try `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`.
3. If that fails, try `uv run python -m megaplan config show`.
4. If that fails, try a version-selected shim such as `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan config show`.
5. Only use bare `megaplan ...` if that exact form already succeeded during this check.
## Triage
A single megaplan can cover as much as 2 weeks of work — don't reflexively split large efforts into multiple plans. Pick the right level based on the task:
- **Skip megaplan**: single-file fixes, bug fixes with clear cause, simple refactors, config changes, adding tests for existing code. Just do it.
- **Light**: multi-file changes with clear scope, well-understood features, straightforward additions. One critique pass, no gate, no review.
- **Standard** (default for megaplan): cross-cutting changes touching many subsystems, unfamiliar codebase areas, ambiguous requirements, changes with high breakage risk, or anything where the plan itself needs debate.
- **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4.

Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan.
## Modes
Megaplan has two output modes, picked with `--mode` at `init`:
- **`--mode code`** (default): the run produces a code diff. Execute workers emit per-task file changes. Use for features, refactors, bug fixes, migrations — anything whose deliverable is source code.
- **`--mode metaplan`** (alias: `--mode doc`): the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <relative/path>` (e.g. `docs/design.md`). The prep, execute, and review phases use authoring-specific prompts; the execute schema uses `sections_written` instead of file changes; auditing reasons about section delivery. Use for design docs, architecture specs, research notes, RFCs, proposals, post-mortems, migration plans — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code. This is the "design-first / preplan" workflow; `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run (both modes have it), not a separate mode.

`--from-doc <relative/path>` works with either mode. The path must be relative to `--project-dir`, must stay inside that directory, and must point to an existing file. When present, `init` imports any `## Settled Decisions` section from that prior doc artifact and stores the source path for later planning and execution context.

All other flags (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, `--hermes`, subagent mode, overrides, step editing) behave identically in both modes. The workflow phases are the same: `prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review`.

1	---
2	name: disk-cleanup
3	description: >
4	  Find and reclaim disk space on this Mac by surveying common bloat
5	  (Docker, dev-repo node_modules / .venv / target dirs, stale git
6	  worktrees, app caches, recordings, Downloads) and deleting only what
7	  the user explicitly approves. Use when the user says they're "running
8	  out of disk", "low on space", asks to "clean up", "find files to
9	  delete", "free up space", or mentions specific bloat categories
10	  (Docker eating disk, dead worktrees, huge node_modules, etc.).
11	---
12	
13	# disk-cleanup
14	
15	A Mac dev-machine cleanup playbook. Runs in two phases: **survey** (read-only,
16	report candidates with sizes) → **execute** (delete only what the user OKs,
17	category by category). Never delete in the survey phase. Never assume the
18	user wants the whole list — wait for explicit picks.
19	
20	## Guiding principles
21	
22	1. **Confirm before deleting.** Always survey first, present sized candidates
23	   grouped by category, and only act on what the user explicitly approves.
24	   Do not infer "yes to all" from a yes on one category.
25	2. **Rebuildable > irreplaceable.** Prioritize things that regenerate on next
26	   `npm install` / `cargo build` / `pip install` / app restart. Treat
27	   recordings, Downloads, and project source as user data — only touch on
28	   explicit instruction.
29	3. **Blast radius matters.** A deleted `node_modules` costs a reinstall. A
30	   deleted `.git` costs work. Show the path before deleting; never glob
31	   blindly across `$HOME`.
32	4. **Process ownership.** "Directory not empty" almost always means a
33	   running process holds a file (Tauri/Cargo, dev server, editor indexer).
34	   Tell the user which dir + likely culprit; don't loop on `rm -rf`.
35	5. **Sort by size, descending.** A user picking from a sized list makes
36	   better calls than a user picking from a name-only list.
37	6. **Report what changed.** After each phase, print before/after `df -h /`
38	   so the user sees real progress, not just "done."
39	
40	## Phase 1 — survey
41	
42	Run these in parallel; they're independent and read-only.
43	
44	### Disk pressure & top directories
45	
46	```bash
47	df -h / | head -2
48	du -sh "$HOME" 2>/dev/null
49	du -sh "$HOME/Documents"/* 2>/dev/null | sort -rh | head -20
50	```
51	
52	### Dev-repo bloat (rebuildable)
53	
54	These regenerate from `package.json` / `Cargo.toml` / `requirements.txt`.
55	Find them, then size each:
56	
57	```bash
58	find "$HOME/Documents" -type d \
59	  \( -name node_modules -o -name .venv -o -name venv \
60	     -o -name .next -o -name target -o -name .turbo \
61	     -o -name dist -o -name build -o -name __pycache__ \
62	     -o -name .cache \) -prune 2>/dev/null \
63	  | while read d; do du -sh "$d" 2>/dev/null; done \
64	  | sort -rh | head -40
65	```
66	
67	The `-prune` is critical — without it, `find` recurses *into* `node_modules`
68	and burns minutes.
69	
70	### Stale git worktrees
71	
72	```bash
73	# Common pattern: bake-off / megaplan worktrees under a workspace dir
74	ls "$HOME/Documents"/*/.megaplan-worktrees/ 2>/dev/null
75	du -sh "$HOME/Documents"/*/.megaplan-worktrees/* 2>/dev/null | sort -rh
76	```
77	
78	To find the owning repo for a worktree, run `git worktree list` from each
79	top-level repo in the workspace and grep for the worktree path.
80	
81	### App caches & data
82	
83	```bash
84	du -sh "$HOME/Library/Caches"/* 2>/dev/null | sort -rh | head -15
85	du -sh "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Slack"/* 2>/dev/null | sort -rh | head -10
86	du -sh "$HOME/Library/Containers/com.docker.docker" 2>/dev/null
87	du -sh "$HOME/Documents/superwhisper/recordings" 2>/dev/null
88	du -sh "$HOME/Downloads" "$HOME/.Trash" 2>/dev/null
89	```
90	
91	### Big single files
92	
93	```bash
94	find "$HOME/Documents" -type f -size +200M 2>/dev/null \
95	  | while read f; do du -h "$f" 2>/dev/null; done \
96	  | sort -rh | head -30
97	```
98	
99	Often these are git pack files (`.git/objects/pack/*.pack`) — don't delete
100	those; `git gc --aggressive` is the right tool. LFS objects under
101	`.git/lfs/objects/` are also load-bearing — leave alone.
102	
103	## Phase 2 — execute (only on explicit approval)
104	
105	Present a categorized list with sizes and the reclaim estimate per category.
106	Wait for the user to pick. Then act category by category in order of
107	biggest-win-first.
108	
109	### Docker (often the largest single win)
110	
111	Docker Desktop must be running for the CLI:
112	
113	```bash
114	docker info >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo OK || echo "Docker Desktop not running"
115	docker system prune -a --volumes -f
116	```
117	
118	**macOS gotcha:** `docker system prune` empties content *inside* the VM,
119	but the host file `~/Library/Containers/com.docker.docker/.../Docker.raw`
120	does NOT auto-shrink. After the prune, tell the user to reclaim it via
121	**Docker Desktop → Settings → Resources** (drag disk image size down /
122	Apply) or `docker run --privileged --pid=host docker/desktop-reclaim-space`.
123	
124	The prune can take minutes and may crash the daemon under disk pressure
125	(EOF on the socket). If it crashes mid-run, a lot was likely freed
126	already — check `docker system df` after the daemon comes back.
127	
128	### Stale git worktrees
129	
130	```bash
131	rm -rf "<workspace>/.megaplan-worktrees"/*
132	# Then prune the dangling refs in each owning repo:
133	for repo in <list of owning repos>; do
134	  (cd "$repo" && git worktree prune)
135	done
136	```
137	
138	`rm -rf` then `git worktree prune` is fine and faster than per-worktree
139	`git worktree remove` when there are dozens.
140	
141	### node_modules / .venv / target
142	
143	Always ask which repos to **exclude** before deleting (active dev work,
144	slow-to-rebuild, or has uncached private deps). Common keepers: anything
145	the user is actively working on, anything with a heavy native build.
146	
147	```bash
148	paths=( "<list>" )
149	for p in "${paths[@]}"; do
150	  [ -e "$p" ] || { echo "skip $p"; continue; }
151	  sz=$(du -sh "$p" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)
152	  rm -rf "$p" && echo "removed $sz  $p"
153	done
154	```
155	
156	If a path errors with "Directory not empty," surface it — don't retry.
157	Tauri/Cargo running, a dev server, or the OS indexer is likely holding
158	files. The user needs to close that, then we can retry.
159	
160	### Slack cache
161	
162	Slack stores both cache (safe to clear) and session/cookies/preferences
163	(do NOT clear — user gets logged out). Target the cache dirs only:
164	
165	```bash
166	rm -rf "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Slack/Cache"/*
167	rm -rf "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Slack/Service Worker/CacheStorage"
168	rm -rf "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Slack/Service Worker/ScriptCache"
169	```
170	
171	Do NOT touch `Cookies`, `Local Storage`, `IndexedDB`, `Preferences`,
172	`Session Storage`, `installation`, or top-level config files.
173	
174	### App-recorded media / Downloads
175	
176	These are user data. Confirm path + total size, then delete contents only
177	on explicit go. Examples: `~/Documents/superwhisper/recordings`,
178	`~/Library/Application Support/<app>/recordings`.
179	
180	For Downloads: never bulk-delete. Offer to list largest files and let the
181	user pick.
182	
183	### Other caches worth mentioning (lower priority)
184	
185	- `brew cleanup` — reclaims old Homebrew downloads.
186	- `~/Library/Caches/pnpm`, `~/Library/Caches/pip` — package manager caches;
187	  safe but rebuild on next install.
188	- `~/Library/Caches/Google` — Chrome cache; clears on next browse.
189	- `git gc --aggressive` in repos with bloated `.git/objects/pack/`.
190	
191	## What NOT to delete
192	
193	- `.git/` directory contents (objects, lfs, refs) — even if huge.
194	- Anything under `~/Library/Mail`, `~/Library/Messages`, `~/Library/Photos`.
195	- App `Preferences`, `Cookies`, `Local Storage`, `IndexedDB`, session DBs.
196	- Source code outside obvious build/dependency dirs.
197	- iCloud-synced files (`~/Library/Mobile Documents`) without explicit OK.
198	- `.env`, credentials, keys, anything in `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.config`.
199	
200	## Reporting format
201	
202	End with a clean before/after:
203	
204	```
205	Free space: <before> → <after>  (~<delta> reclaimed)
206	
207	Reclaimed:
208	  - Docker: <X> GiB
209	  - Worktrees: <Y> GiB
210	  - node_modules/.venv: <Z> GiB
211	  - <category>: ...
212	
213	Remaining opportunities (not done):
214	  - <category>: ~<size>  — <reason / what user needs to do>
215	```
216	
217	If Docker was pruned but `Docker.raw` wasn't compacted, list it under
218	"Remaining opportunities" with the Settings → Resources instructions.
219
```

  "content": "---\nname: cleanup-loose-branches\ndescription: >\n  Survey every place loose work hides in a git repo — local branches not\n  merged to main, worktrees (including .megaplan-worktrees and bare-style\n  setups), stashes, detached HEADs, and remote branches on GitHub — then\n  classify each as merge / delete / inspect / keep with reasoning, and act\n  only on what the user approves. Use when the user says \"clean up loose\n  branches\", \"prune branches\", \"what branches can I delete\", \"clean up\n  worktrees\", \"review my stashes\", \"what's lying around in this repo\",\n  or asks for branch / worktree / stash housekeeping.\n---\n\n# cleanup-loose-branches\n\nTwo phases: **survey** (read-only, every hiding place, classified) →\n**execute** (delete / merge only what the user OKs, bucket by bucket).\nNever delete in the survey phase. Never collapse \"yes to bucket A\" into\n\"yes to bucket B.\"\n\nThe deliverable of phase 1 is a single, sortable table of every loose\nref with a recommendation and one-line reason — that's the artifact the\nuser will actually act on. Don't bury it under prose.\n\n## Guiding principles\n\n1. **Loose ≠ deletable.** A branch with unmerged commits may be the\n   user's in-progress feature. Recommend, don't reflex-delete. Default\n   to *inspect* over *delete* when signals conflict.\n2. **Squash-merges are invisible to `--merged`.** `git branch --merged main`\n   only catches fast-forward / true-merge ancestry. Most modern repos\n   squash-merge, which rewrites SHAs. Use `git cherry main <branch>` and\n   PR-state lookups to catch those.\n3. **Worktrees pin branches.** A branch checked out in another worktree\n   refuses `git branch -d`. Survey worktrees first so the table can flag\n   \"pinned by worktree at <path>\" instead of looking like a mysterious\n   error later.\n4. **Stashes silently expire.** Never run `git stash clear`. Drop\n   stashes one at a time after showing the user the diff stat.\n5. **Remote ≠ local.** GitHub branches survive local deletion (and vice\n   versa). Survey both sides and present them as paired rows where they\n   match; orphans on each side get their own rows.\n6. **PR state is the strongest signal.** A branch with a *merged* PR is\n   almost always safe to delete locally. A branch with an *open* PR is\n   almost always a keeper. Pull PR state up front via `gh`.\n7. **Show ahead/behind, not just \"unmerged.\"** \"5 ahead, 0 behind, last\n   commit 4 months ago, no PR\" tells the user what to do. \"Unmerged\"\n   doesn't.\n\n## Phase 1 — survey\n\nRun these in parallel; all read-only. Resolve the main branch name\nfirst (`main` vs `master` vs `trunk`) — don't hardcode:\n\n```bash\nMAIN=$(git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD 2>/dev/null \\\n       | sed 's@^refs/remotes/origin/@@')\nMAIN=${MAIN:-main}\ngit fetch --all --prune --quiet 2>/dev/null\n```\n\n### Worktrees (do this FIRST — needed to annotate branches)\n\n```bash\ngit worktree list --porcelain\n```\n\nBuild a map `branch → worktree path`. Any branch in that map gets a\n\"pinned: <path>\" note on its row and cannot be `git branch -d`'d\nwithout first removing the worktree.\n\nAlso flag worktrees whose directory no longer exists on disk (`prunable`\nin porcelain output) — those just need `git worktree prune`.\n\nLook in common worktree-stash locations even outside the repo:\n\n```bash\nls -d \"$PWD/.megaplan-worktrees\"/* 2>/dev/null\nls -d \"$PWD/../\"*\"/.megaplan-worktrees\"/* 2>/dev/null   # sibling repos\nfind \"$PWD\" -maxdepth 3 -type d -name '.worktrees' 2>/dev/null\n```\n\nFor each worktree path: get its branch, ahead/behind vs `$MAIN`, and\nwhether it has uncommitted changes (`git -C <path> status --porcelain`).\nUncommitted changes in a worktree are the **highest-risk** thing to\nlose — flag them red.\n\n### Local branches\n\n```bash\ngit for-each-ref \\\n  --format='%(refname:short)|%(committerdate:iso8601)|%(committerdate:relative)|%(upstream:short)|%(upstream:track)|%(objectname:short)|%(contents:subject)' \\\n  refs/heads/\n```\n\nFor each branch (skip `$MAIN`):\n\n- `ahead/behind $MAIN`: `git rev-list --left-right --count $MAIN...<br>`\n- `merged?`: `git merge-base --is-ancestor <br> $MAIN` (true-merge ancestry)\n- `cherry-merged?` (squash detection): `git cherry $MAIN <br> | grep -c '^+'`\n  → if zero `+` lines, every commit's patch already exists on `$MAIN`\n  (i.e. squash-merged or rebased in)\n- `pinned`: from the worktree map above\n- `upstream gone?`: `[gone]` in `%(upstream:track)` means the remote\n  branch was deleted (usually because the PR was merged + auto-deleted)\n\n### Stashes\n\n```bash\ngit stash list --format='%gd|%cr|%s'\nfor s in $(git stash list --format='%gd'); do\n  echo \"=== $s ===\"\n  git stash show --stat \"$s\"\ndone\n```\n\nStashes get their own bucket — never folded into the branch table.\nFor each, capture: which branch it was created on, age, files touched,\ntotal line delta.\n\n### Detached HEAD commits in reflog (rescue-able orphans)\n\n```bash\ngit reflog --all | head -50\ngit fsck --unreachable --no-reflogs 2>/dev/null | grep '^unreachable commit'\n```\n\nThese are commits not reachable from any branch. Usually safe to ignore\n(they'll be GC'd), but if the user has lost work, this is where to look.\nDon't list every unreachable commit in the main table — mention the\ncount and offer to dig in only if it's non-trivial.\n\n### Remote branches (GitHub)\n\n```bash\ngh repo view --json nameWithOwner -q .nameWithOwner   # confirm gh works\ngit branch -r --format='%(refname:short)|%(committerdate:iso8601)|%(committerdate:relative)'\n```\n\nThen pull PR state for each remote branch in one shot — much faster\nthan per-branch lookups:\n\n```bash\ngh pr list --state all --limit 200 \\\n  --json number,state,headRefName,baseRefName,title,updatedAt,isDraft,mergedAt,author \\\n  > /tmp/prs.json\n```\n\nJoin PR state onto remote branches by `headRefName`. A branch may have\nmultiple PRs (closed + reopened); the **most recent** one wins.\n\n### Uncommitted work in the current checkout\n\n```bash\ngit status --porcelain\ngit diff --stat\ngit diff --stat --cached\n```\n\nNever include this in delete recommendations — it's just context so\nthe user knows the current state of the working tree before doing\nbranch surgery.\n\n## Classification rules\n\nFor each row, pick exactly one recommendation. Order matters — first\nmatching rule wins:\n\n| Signal | Recommendation | Reason text |\n|---|---|---|\n| Worktree has uncommitted changes | **inspect** | \"uncommitted work in worktree at <path>\" |\n| Open PR (not draft) | **keep** | \"open PR #N: <title>\" |\n| Draft PR | **keep** | \"draft PR #N — still in progress\" |\n| Merged PR + local branch exists | **delete (local)** | \"PR #N merged <date>, safe to drop locally\" |\n| Merged PR + remote branch exists | **delete (remote)** | \"PR #N merged, remote branch is residual\" |\n| Closed PR (not merged), branch >30d old | **delete** | \"PR #N closed without merging <date>\" |\n| `cherry-merged` (zero `+` in `git cherry`) | **delete** | \"squash-merged into $MAIN, no unique commits\" |\n| `--merged` ancestor of $MAIN | **delete** | \"fully merged into $MAIN\" |\n| `upstream: gone` and 0 ahead local | **delete (local)** | \"remote deleted, nothing local to lose\" |\n| Pinned by worktree | **inspect** | \"checked out in worktree at <path>\" |\n| Last commit >90d, no PR, ahead of $MAIN | **inspect** | \"stale (<N>d), <X> ahead, no PR — abandoned?\" |\n| Last commit <14d, ahead of $MAIN | **keep** | \"recent work (<N>d ago), <X> ahead\" |\n| Anything else | **inspect** | \"<X> ahead / <Y> behind, last commit <N>d ago\" |\n\nBe conservative: when two rules could apply, prefer the more cautious\nrecommendation. *delete* requires positive evidence; *inspect* is the\nfallback.\n\n### Fit-to-current-code signal (optional, when the user wants depth)\n\nFor an *inspect*-tier branch, gauge how well it would land today:\n\n```bash\ngit merge-tree --write-tree $(git merge-base $MAIN <br>) $MAIN <br> \\\n  2>&1 | grep -c '<<<<<<<'   # conflict count, no working tree touched\n```\n\nNewer git (2.38+) supports `git merge-tree --name-only` for a conflict\nfile list without creating an actual merge. If a branch would have\nzero conflicts and its diff is small, upgrade the recommendation note\nto \"would merge cleanly\". If it's 20+ conflicting files, note\n\"heavy conflicts — likely superseded\".\n\nDon't run this for every branch by default — it's `O(branches)` real\nwork. Only run it for the *inspect* tier, or when the user asks\n\"which of these would still merge.\"\n\n## Phase 1 output format\n\nOne table, sorted by recommendation (delete → inspect → keep), then by\nage descending. Columns:\n\n```\nREC      WHERE           BRANCH                   AHEAD/BEHIND  AGE     PR        REASON\ndelete   local+remote    fix/typo-in-readme       0/0           merged  #412 ✓   PR merged 2026-03-02, safe to drop\ndelete   local           experiment/old-idea      8/142         11mo    none     stale, no PR, far behind main\ninspect  worktree:.mp.../wt-42  feat/payments     23/4          3d      none     uncommitted work in worktree\ninspect  local           refactor/auth            5/0           45d     #501 ✗   PR closed unmerged 2026-04-01\nkeep     local+remote    feat/new-dashboard       12/2          2d      #530 ◐   open PR\n```\n\nSymbols: `✓` merged, `✗` closed, `◐` open, `◌` draft. Keep the table\nnarrow enough to read in a normal terminal — truncate branch names with\nellipsis if needed, but never the recommendation or reason.\n\nBelow the table, list:\n\n- **Stashes**: numbered list with age, base branch, files touched, line delta\n- **Prunable worktrees**: just paths (one-line `git worktree prune` fix)\n- **Uncommitted work in current checkout** (one-line summary, if any)\n- **Counts**: \"12 delete-able / 5 inspect / 8 keep / 3 stashes / 2 prunable worktrees\"\n\nEnd with: \"Which bucket should I act on? Reply with bucket name(s) or\nspecific branches.\" Do not proceed to phase 2 without explicit picks.\n\n## Phase 2 — execute (only on explicit approval)\n\nAct bucket by bucket, in this order (lowest blast radius first):\n\n### Prunable worktrees\n\n```bash\ngit worktree prune --verbose\n```\n\nJust dangling metadata for directories that no longer exist. Always\nsafe. Do this first to clean the slate.\n\n### Delete local branches (already-merged)\n\nFor each:\n\n```bash\ngit branch -d <branch>     # -d, not -D — refuses if not merged\n```\n\nIf `-d` refuses for a branch the user explicitly approved, surface the\nerror and ask before falling back to `-D`. Never silently force-delete.\n\nA branch pinned to a worktree needs the worktree removed first:\n\n```bash\ngit worktree remove <path>   # add --force only if user OKs losing changes\ngit branch -d <branch>\n```\n\n### Delete remote branches\n\n```bash\ngit push origin --delete <branch>\n```\n\nGroup these into one batched push when there are many:\n\n```bash\ngit push origin --delete <br1> <br2> <br3>\n```\n\nIf the GitHub repo has \"automatically delete head branches\" enabled,\nmany merged-PR branches will already be gone — `git fetch --prune`\ncatches up locally. Surface this if you see lots of `[gone]` upstream\ntracks; the user may want to enable the setting and skip this bucket\nin future runs.\n\n### Worktrees with no uncommitted changes\n\n```bash\ngit worktree remove <path>\n```\n\nThis also drops the branch if it was created with `git worktree add -b`\nand is not checked out elsewhere. If the worktree has uncommitted\nchanges, **never** use `--force` without an explicit \"yes I want to\nlose those changes\" — show the diff first.\n\n### Stashes\n\nDrop one at a time after re-confirming each:\n\n```bash\ngit stash drop stash@{N}\n```\n\nNever `git stash clear` — that's irreversible bulk deletion. If the\nuser wants to keep one but get it out of the stash list, suggest\n`git stash branch <name> stash@{N}` to materialize it as a real branch.\n\n### Force-delete unmerged branches\n\nOnly on explicit \"yes, I want to lose those commits\" per branch. Show\nthe user the commits that will be unreachable first:\n\n```bash\ngit log --oneline $MAIN..<branch>\ngit branch -D <branch>\n```\n\nMention: the commits remain reachable via reflog for ~90 days, so this\nis recoverable in the short term.\n\n## What NOT to do\n\n- **Never** `git stash clear`, `git reflog expire --expire=now --all`,\n  `git gc --prune=now`, or `git push --force` as part of cleanup.\n- **Never** delete a branch with uncommitted changes in its worktree\n  without showing the diff and getting per-branch approval.\n- **Never** force-delete (`-D`) a branch the user only said \"yes\" to\n  in a different bucket — `-d` first, surface the refusal, ask again.\n- **Never** delete a branch named like `release/*`, `prod/*`,\n  `staging/*`, `hotfix/*`, or matching `gh api repos/:owner/:repo\n  --jq .default_branch` — flag those as **keep** regardless of merge\n  status and call it out: \"looks like a release/protected branch.\"\n- **Never** rewrite history (`rebase`, `filter-branch`, `reset --hard`\n  on shared branches) as part of cleanup — that's a different request.\n\n## Reporting format\n\nAfter execute, print:\n\n```\nCleaned up:\n  - Local branches deleted:   <N>   (<list or \"+ N more\">)\n  - Remote branches deleted:  <N>\n  - Worktrees removed:        <N>\n  - Stashes dropped:          <N>\n  - Worktree metadata pruned: <N>\n\nKept:\n  - <N> branches with open/draft PRs\n  - <N> branches flagged inspect (not yet decided)\n\nStill to decide:\n  - <bucket>: <count>  — <one-line why it was held back>\n```\n\nIf the user only approved part of the *delete* bucket, the held-back\nrows go under \"Still to decide\" with the reason (\"user excluded\",\n\"uncommitted changes\", \"refused -d, needs -D\").\n\n## When to skip this skill\n\n- The user wants to clean up *files* on disk, not git refs → use\n  `disk-cleanup` instead.\n- The user wants to rewrite history or recover lost commits → that's\n  reflog / `git fsck` work, not branch hygiene.\n- The repo isn't a git repo (`git rev-parse --git-dir` fails) → say so\n  and stop.\n"
}
```

- `~/.claude/skills/cleanup-loose-branches/SKILL.md`
- `~/.codex/skills/cleanup-loose-branches/SKILL.md`

**How it works:**

**Phase 1 — survey (read-only).** Sweeps every hiding place in parallel:
- Worktrees first (so branches can be flagged "pinned by worktree at …") — including `.megaplan-worktrees/*` and sibling-repo variants
- Local branches with `for-each-ref` + `ahead/behind` + squash-merge detection via `git cherry` (catches squash-merges that `--merged` misses)
- Stashes (own bucket, never folded in)
- Reflog / `git fsck --unreachable` for orphaned commits
- Remote branches joined to GitHub PR state via one batched `gh pr list` call
- Uncommitted work in the current checkout

**Classification rules** turn signals into one of: `keep` / `delete` / `inspect`. Order-sensitive — open PR wins over everything; uncommitted-in-worktree forces `inspect`; squash-merged is detected even without ancestry. Default is conservative (`inspect` over `delete` on conflict). Optional fit-to-current-code check uses `git merge-tree` to predict conflicts without touching the working tree.

**Phase 1 output** is one sortable table (rec / where / branch / ahead-behind / age / PR / reason) plus separate sections for stashes, prunable worktrees, and uncommitted work — sized counts at the bottom and a "which bucket?" prompt. Nothing happens without explicit picks.

**Phase 2 — execute** acts bucket-by-bucket in lowest-blast-radius order: prune metadata → delete merged local → delete remote → remove worktrees → drop stashes one at a time → force-delete only with per-branch "yes, lose those commits." Hard guardrails: never `git stash clear`, never silent `-D`, never touch `release/*`/`prod/*`/`staging/*`/default-branch even if "merged," never `--force` a worktree with uncommitted changes without showing the diff.

Try it with "clean up loose branches in this repo" in either harness.

## Sense-check findings on the skill itself

Running it surfaced three things worth fixing in `SKILL.md`:

1. **Rule ordering bug.** The classification table fires `Merged PR + local branch exists → delete (local)` **before** `cherry-merged`. But `arnold-merge/sprint-1-foundation` has a *merged* PR #15 **and** 3 unique commits past the merge — straight "delete" would lose those commits. The rule needs an `AND cherry +0` guard, or `cherry-merged` needs to outrank merged-PR.

2. **"Local ahead of own remote" isn't shown.** `desloppify/code-health` is `[ahead 6]` of `origin/desloppify/code-health` but `cherry +0` of main — meaning the 6 commits are already squash-merged and the local-vs-remote skew is a red herring. The table currently shows ahead/behind vs main only; for branches that aren't ancestors of main, the upstream-track field should appear in the reason or as a sub-row.

3. **Stash ↔ inspect-tier branch linkage is buried.** Stash@{1} sits on a branch we recommend `inspect`, and stash@{2}/{3} sit on branches we recommend `delete`. Currently stashes are a flat list under the table — but "deleting branch X also leaves a stash that was based on it" is exactly the footgun the skill exists to catch. Should mark stashes whose base branch is in the *delete* bucket as `inspect` until that branch is resolved.

Also one nice signal it caught correctly: the `rescue/tiebreaker-*` family — local names diverge from the PR headRefNames (`tiebreaker-*`, no `rescue/` prefix), so the PR-state join failed, but `git cherry` correctly classified them as squash-merged. The skill's belt-and-braces design (PR state *and* cherry-merge) is doing what it was meant to.

Want me to patch the SKILL.md with those three fixes, or proceed to phase 2 and start cleaning up?

Order matters — first\nmatching rule wins:\n\n| Signal | Recommendation | Reason text |\n|---|---|---|\n| Worktree has uncommitted changes | **inspect** | \"uncommitted work in worktree at <path>\" |\n| Open PR (not draft) | **keep** | \"open PR #N: <title>\" |\n| Draft PR | **keep** | \"draft PR #N — still in progress\" |\n| Merged PR **AND** `cherry +0` (no unique commits) | **delete (local)** | \"PR #N merged <date>, no unique commits past merge\" |\n| Merged PR **BUT** `cherry +N` (unique commits exist) | **inspect** | \"PR #N merged BUT <N> unique commits remain post-merge — review before dropping\" |\n| Closed PR (not merged) **AND** `cherry +0`, >30d old | **delete** | \"PR #N closed without merging, no unique commits\" |\n| Closed PR (not merged) **AND** `cherry +N` | **inspect** | \"PR #N closed unmerged, <N> unique commits would be lost\" |\n| `cherry-merged` (zero `+` in `git cherry`) | **delete** | \"squash-merged into $MAIN, no unique commits\" |\n| `--merged` ancestor of $MAIN | **delete** | \"fully merged into $MAIN\" |\n| `upstream: gone` and 0 ahead local | **delete (local)** | \"remote deleted, nothing local to lose\" |\n| Pinned by worktree | **inspect** | \"checked out in worktree at <path>\" |\n| Last commit >90d, no PR, ahead of $MAIN | **inspect** | \"stale (<N>d), <X> ahead, no PR — abandoned?\" |\n| Last commit <14d, ahead of $MAIN | **keep** | \"recent work (<N>d ago), <X> ahead\" |\n| Anything else | **inspect** | \"<X> ahead / <Y> behind, last commit <N>d ago\" |\n\n**The `cherry +N` guard is load-bearing.** A merged PR does not mean\nthe branch has nothing unique left — people frequently push fixups after\nthe merge, or the PR landed an older revision. `cherry +0` is the only\nsignal that says \"every commit's patch is on main.\" Without this guard,\nthe merged-PR rule silently deletes those post-merge fixups. Always\ncompute `git cherry $MAIN <br>` before classifying a merged-PR branch.\n\nBe conservative: when two rules could apply, prefer the more cautious\nrecommendation. *delete* requires positive evidence; *inspect* is the\nfallback.\n\n### Local-vs-own-remote skew\n\nWhen a branch is `--merged` into $MAIN but its `upstream:track` says\n`[ahead N]` (local has N commits its own remote doesn't), it usually\nmeans those N commits were squash-merged into $MAIN and the remote\nbranch is just stale. `cherry +0` confirms it. Surface this in the\nreason as: \"ancestor of $MAIN; also N ahead of own remote\n(squash-merged), remote is residual\" so the user understands why the\nlocal-vs-remote diff exists and that the remote branch is also\ndelete-able. Don't quietly drop the upstream-track signal — it's\nexactly the kind of mismatch users want explained."

+def _latest_versioned_artifact(plan_dir: Path | None, prefix: str) -> Path | None:
+    """Return the highest-numbered versioned artifact (``<prefix>v<N>.json``)."""
+    if plan_dir is None:
+        return None
+    try:
+        candidates = [
+            p for p in plan_dir.iterdir()
+            if p.name.startswith(prefix) and p.suffix == ".json"
+        ]
+    except OSError:
+        return None
+    if not candidates:
+        return None
+
+    def _version(path: Path) -> int:
+        stem = path.stem  # drop .json
+        try:
+            return int(stem.split("v")[-1])
+        except (ValueError, IndexError):
+            return -1
+
+    candidates.sort(key=_version)
+    return candidates[-1] if candidates else None
+
+
+def _read_unresolved_flag_ids(plan_dir: Path | None) -> list[str]:
+    """Best-effort list of unresolved flag IDs from the latest gate signals.
+
+    Falls back to the latest critique artifact if no gate_signals_v*.json
+    exists yet (e.g. ESCALATE arrived from a non-gate path). Returns ``[]``
+    on any read/parse error — callers must treat that as "no flags known"
+    and synthesize a generic note rather than crashing.
+    """
+    sources = [
+        _latest_versioned_artifact(plan_dir, "gate_signals_v"),
+        _latest_versioned_artifact(plan_dir, "critique_v"),
+    ]
+    for path in sources:
+        if path is None:
+            continue
+        try:
+            with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as handle:
+                payload = json.load(handle)
+        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
+            continue
+        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
+            continue
+        # gate_signals_v*.json uses [REDACTED]; critique_v*.json
+        # uses "flags". Either may be missing on truncated artifacts.
+        for key in ("unresolved_flags", "flags"):
+            flags = payload.get(key)
+            if isinstance(flags, list) and flags:
+                ids = [
+                    str(f.get("id"))
+                    for f in flags
+                    if isinstance(f, dict) and isinstance(f.get("id"), str) and f.get("id")
+                ]
+                if ids:
+                    return ids
+    return []
+
+
+def _synthesize_add_note_text(
+    plan_dir: Path | None,
+    *,
+    iteration: int,
+    attempt: int,
+) -> str:
+    """Build a non-empty `--note` string for an unattended add-note dispatch.
+
+    The orchestrator only reaches `override add-note` when the
+    critique→revise→gate loop has failed to converge and the gate has
+    punted to a human. Without a human, the auto-driver records why it
+    is advancing anyway: the unresolved flag IDs (if readable) and the
+    iteration/attempt counters so audits can spot loops.
+    """
+    flag_ids = _read_unresolved_flag_ids(plan_dir)
+    if flag_ids:
+        # Cap the inline list — strict-notes mode rejects giant blobs.
+        head = ", ".join(flag_ids[:10])
+        suffix = f" (+{len(flag_ids) - 10} more)" if len(flag_ids) > 10 else ""
+        flags_part = f"; unresolved=[{head}{suffix}]"
+    else:
+        flags_part = "; unresolved=[unknown]"
+    return (
+        f"auto: critique loop unresolved at iter {iteration} "
+        f"(add-note attempt {attempt}){flags_part}; advancing without human"
+    )
+
+
+def _build_override_add_note_command(
+    plan: str,
+    plan_dir: Path | None,
+    *,
+    iteration: int,
+    attempt: int,
+) -> list[str]:
+    """Construct the full argv for ``megaplan override add-note ...``.
+
+    Matches the CLI contract enforced by ``cli.py::_validate_override_args``:
+    requires both ``--plan`` and a non-empty ``--note``. Without ``--note``
+    the subcommand fails with ``invalid_args`` and the auto-driver retries
+    the same broken call until stall detection kills the run.

diff --git a/megaplan/workers.py b/megaplan/workers.py
index 11b9974b..68a5f6d8 100644
--- a/megaplan/workers.py
+++ b/megaplan/workers.py
@@ -424,6 +424,31 @@ def _trusted_container() -> bool:
     }
 
 
+def _sandbox_fingerprint(work_dir: Path | str) -> str:
+    """Return a stable hash of the sandbox-affecting inputs for codex.
+
+    Captures every input that would change codex's effective sandbox
+    between invocations:
+
+    - ``MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER`` (toggles ``--dangerously-bypass-...``)
+    - ``work_dir`` (appears in ``-C`` and in
+      ``sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots``)
+
+    The hash is stored on each session entry when it is created; at resume
+    time we refuse to reuse a session whose fingerprint no longer matches.
+    This prevents the silent drift where an operator sets
+    ``MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1`` *after* a session was created and
+    codex keeps using the locked-in (broken) sandbox forever.
+    """
+    trusted = "1" if _trusted_container() else "0"
+    try:
+        work_resolved = str(Path(work_dir).resolve())
+    except Exception:
+        work_resolved = str(work_dir)
+    payload = f"trusted={trusted}\nwork_dir={work_resolved}\n"
+    return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:16]
+
+
 def _codex_child_env() -> dict[str, str]:
     env = os.environ.copy()
     # Nested Codex workers should not inherit the parent Codex session state.

**Five unmerged production bug-fixes** for the bakeoff path — Fireworks 4096-token streaming, sandbox enforcement, doc-mode drift, files_missing severity, batch progress overlay. **Confidence: very high.** Drop this branch and you lose all five.

- `_sandbox_fingerprint()` helper in `workers.py` (hashes trusted-container + work_dir to refuse stale sessions)

The `_sandbox_fingerprint` logic looks genuinely useful — it prevents the silent-stale-sandbox bug described in the comment. **Confidence:** moderate that this is worth landing. ### `stash@{3}` (WIP tiebreaker test file) → **REVIVE, DON'T DROP**

The stash adds a **brand-new 589-line `tests/test_tiebreaker_trigger.py`** that does **not exist on main**. === Full commit c2bbc729 ===
commit c2bbc7297a55af45a0ef20c7a16479f3bf8d1920
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Mon May 4 23:38:24 2026 +0200

    fix(hermes): force streaming for Fireworks calls when max_tokens > 4096
    
    Fireworks rejects requests with `max_tokens > 4096` unless `stream=true`,
    returning HTTP 400.  Megaplan's hermes worker uses 8192 (16384 for execute)
    to give thinking models room for reasoning + output, which tripped this on
    every `hermes:fireworks:*` phase call.
    
    Flip on streaming for Fireworks-prefixed models when max_tokens crosses
    the threshold by registering a no-op stream callback on `run_conversation`.
    That trips run_agent's existing streaming path, which already reassembles
    the streamed response into the same shape the non-streaming path returns —
    so callers and downstream parsing are unchanged.  Streaming stays internal
    to the worker; nothing about phase outputs or the WorkerResult shape leaks
    streaming semantics upward.
    
    The fix is provider-scoped: OpenRouter / DeepSeek-direct / MiniMax don't
    have this constraint and keep using the non-streaming wire format.  The
    fallback paths (MiniMax → OpenRouter) are unaffected because they re-evaluate
    streaming against the new model name.
    
    The summary-prompt fallback inside `parse_agent_output` (used when a model
    finishes with tool calls but no JSON) also forwards the streaming kwargs so
    those follow-up calls don't hit the same Fireworks 400.
    
    Tests cover (a) streaming gets forced for Fireworks at 8192 max_tokens but
    not for other providers or at <=4096, (b) a real Fireworks 400 propagates
    as a hard failure rather than silently degrading to empty output, and
    (c) the summary-prompt fallback keeps the streaming kwarg.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

 megaplan/hermes_worker.py                       |  73 ++++-
 megaplan/parallel_critique.py                   |  24 +-

    to give thinking models room for reasoning + output, which tripped this on
    every `hermes:fireworks:*` phase call.
    
    Flip on streaming for Fireworks-prefixed models when max_tokens crosses
    the threshold by registering a no-op stream callback on `run_conversation`.
    That trips run_agent's existing streaming path, which already reassembles
    the streamed response into the same shape the non-streaming path returns —
    so callers and downstream parsing are unchanged.  Streaming stays internal
    to the worker; nothing about phase outputs or the WorkerResult shape leaks
    streaming semantics upward.
    
    The fix is provider-scoped: OpenRouter / DeepSeek-direct / MiniMax don't
    have this constraint and keep using the non-streaming wire format.  The
    fallback paths (MiniMax → OpenRouter) are unaffected because they re-evaluate
    streaming against the new model name.
    
    The summary-prompt fallback inside `parse_agent_output` (used when a model
    finishes with tool calls but no JSON) also forwards the streaming kwargs so
    those follow-up calls don't hit the same Fireworks 400.
    
    Tests cover (a) streaming gets forced for Fireworks at 8192 max_tokens but
    not for other providers or at <=4096, (b) a real Fireworks 400 propagates

diff --git a/megaplan/hermes_worker.py b/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
index 4a8f422c..c018ef3b 100644
--- a/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
+++ b/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
@@ -37,6 +37,48 @@ def _import_hermes_runtime():
     return AIAgent, SessionDB
 
 
+# Fireworks rejects requests with `max_tokens > 4096` unless `stream=true`.
+# When that threshold applies we flip the call to streaming and let
+# run_agent's streaming path reassemble the response into the same shape the
+# rest of megaplan expects.  Streaming lives entirely inside the worker —
+# downstream callers never see streaming semantics.
+_FIREWORKS_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS = 4096
+
+
+def _no_op_stream(_text: str) -> None:
+    """Sentinel callback that activates run_agent's streaming path.
+
+    AIAgent decides between streaming and non-streaming based on whether a
+    stream consumer is registered.  We don't need the deltas, just the side
+    effect of forcing `stream=True` on the underlying chat.completions call.
+    """
+    return None
+_no_op_stream._megaplan_force_stream = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
+
+
+def _provider_requires_streaming(model: str | None, max_tokens: int | None) -> bool:
+    """Return True when this provider/max_tokens pair must use streaming.
+
+    Today only Fireworks has this constraint (max_tokens > 4096 must stream).
+    Other providers (OpenRouter, DeepSeek-direct, MiniMax, etc.) accept
+    non-streamed high-max_tokens requests, so we keep the opt-in narrow.
+    """
+    if not model or not isinstance(model, str):
+        return False
+    if not model.startswith("fireworks:"):
+        return False
+    if max_tokens is None:
+        return False
+    return max_tokens > _FIREWORKS_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS
+
+
+def _streaming_run_kwargs(model: str | None, max_tokens: int | None) -> dict:
+    """Build the run_conversation kwargs needed to force streaming when required."""
+    if _provider_requires_streaming(model, max_tokens):
+        return {"stream_callback": _no_op_stream}
+    return {}
+
+
 def _toolsets_for_phase(phase: str) -> list[str] | None:
     """Return toolsets for a given megaplan phase.
 
@@ -109,8 +151,15 @@ def parse_agent_output(
     step: str,
     project_dir: Path,
     plan_dir: Path,
+    run_kwargs: dict | None = None,
 ) -> tuple[dict, str]:
-    """Parse a Hermes agent result into a structured payload."""
+    """Parse a Hermes agent result into a structured payload.
+
+    ``run_kwargs`` is forwarded to any follow-up ``agent.run_conversation``
+    calls (template / summary fallbacks) so providers that require streaming
+    (e.g. Fireworks at high max_tokens) keep streaming on those calls too.

## ✅ Completed (no code broken)

### Created and pushed
- **`per-milestone-robustness-rebase`** + **PR #28** → https://github.com/peteromallet/megaplan/pull/28

- **`sandbox-fingerprint`** — 39-line `_sandbox_fingerprint` helper + `sandbox_hash` typed field from stash@{2}

## ⚠️ Surfaced — needs your hand resolution (3 commits)

Three commits from per-milestone-robustness wouldn't auto-merge safely. They're still on the original branch (`megaplan/per-milestone-robustness-20260503` — preserved both locally and on origin):

| SHA | Subject | Blocker |
|---|---|---|
| `c2bbc729` | fix(hermes): force streaming Fireworks >4096 tokens | `hermes_worker.py` conflict — main caps tokens for Qwen; commit gates streaming on token cap. Both correct, need to coexist. |
| `a0ed9f51` | fix(hermes): enforce project_dir sandbox | `hermes_worker.py` conflict — main refactored the same area. New `megaplan/sandbox.py` file is fine, just the wire-in conflicts. |
| `2a60d147` | fix(drift): escalate severity on files_missing | Applies cleanly, but breaks `test_execute_deduplicates_task_updates_and_blocks_incomplete_coverage` because the test asserts an exact summary string that's now correctly prefixed with `[scope_drift=low]`. Test needs updating. |

Recover with: `git cherry-pick c2bbc729 a0ed9f51 2a60d147` and resolve.

## 📋 Skill updates applied during this run

Three improvements landed in `~/.claude/skills/cleanup-loose-branches/SKILL.md` and `~/.codex/skills/cleanup-loose-branches/SKILL.md`:

1. Cherry-guarded merged-PR rule (caught the `arnold-merge/sprint-1-foundation` 3-post-merge-commits case)

4. "Strong recommendation + deploy subagents for depth" mandate (the per-milestone-robustness verdict needed subagent investigation)
5. Per-item walk-through framing (not bucket-batched, per your guidance)

- **PR #28**: ready for review/merge. The 3 deferred commits can be tackled after this lands.
- **`tiebreaker-tests` branch**: starts from an old base (4 weeks behind main); needs rebase + test-rot fix before opening as a tests-only PR.

- **`docs/megaplan-rubric.md`**: your in-progress edits restored intact, untouched throughout.

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

from megaplan.prompts import create_claude_prompt, create_codex_prompt, create_hermes_prompt
from megaplan.types import (
    CliError,
    PlanState,
    STATE_AWAITING_HUMAN,
    STATE_PLANNED,
    STATE_TIEBREAKER_PENDING,
    STATE_TIEBREAKER_READY,
    StepResponse,
)
from megaplan._core import atomic_write_json, load_flag_registry, load_plan_locked, now_utc, read_json, require_state, save_flag_registry

def _build_tiebreaker_reprompt(
    agent_type: str, state: PlanState, plan_dir: Path, *, root: Path,
) -> str:
    if agent_type == "claude":
        base_prompt = create_claude_prompt("gate", state, plan_dir, root=root)
    elif agent_type == "hermes":
        base_prompt = create_hermes_prompt("gate", state, plan_dir, root=root)
    else:
        base_prompt = create_codex_prompt("gate", state, plan_dir, root=root)
    addendum = (
        "You recommended TIEBREAKER but no flag shows mechanical recurrence signal "
        "(addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2, or >=2 flags across >=2 iterations). "
        "Either identify specific flag(s) with iteration history or pick ITERATE."

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Plan version helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def latest_plan_record(state: PlanState) -> PlanVersionRecord:
    plan_versions = state["plan_versions"]
    if not plan_versions:
        raise CliError("missing_plan_version", "No plan version exists yet")
    return plan_versions[-1]


def latest_plan_path(plan_dir: Path, state: PlanState) -> Path:
    return plan_dir / latest_plan_record(state)["file"]

  "new_string": "def _seed_plan_file(plan_dir: Path, *, iteration: int = 1) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Seed the artifacts needed for _validate_tiebreaker's no-signal path.\n\n    The handler reads:\n    - plan_v1.md / plan_v1.meta.json (from state['plan_versions'][-1])\n    - gate_signals_v<iteration>.json (current iteration's signals)\n    - critique_v<iteration>.json (current iteration's critique)\n    Each is given a minimal stub here; tests that exercise content-sensitive\n    paths should overwrite with realistic fixtures.\n    \"\"\"\n    (plan_dir / \"plan_v1.md\").write_text(\"# stub plan\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    (plan_dir / \"plan_v1.meta.json\").write_text(\n        json.dumps({\"settled_decisions\": []}), encoding=\"utf-8\"\n    )\n    for ver in range(1, iteration + 1):\n        (plan_dir / f\"gate_signals_v{ver}.json\").write_text(\n            json.dumps({\"unresolved_flags\": [], \"flags\": []}), encoding=\"utf-8\"\n        )\n        (plan_dir / f\"critique_v{ver}.json\").write_text(\n            json.dumps({\"flags\": []}), encoding=\"utf-8\"\n        )"
}

=== Full commit content ===
commit a0ed9f51f80021444e2fbd1eb80bca57f0de48a0
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Tue May 5 01:59:04 2026 +0200

    fix(hermes): enforce project_dir sandbox at the tool layer
    
    Bakeoff arms run each profile in its own worktree under
    .megaplan-worktrees/<plan>/<profile>/. The worktree path is communicated
    to the model via a "Project directory: <abs path>" line in the execute
    prompt, but the tool layer never enforced it — so when a model resolved
    a conflicting "Project: ..." line in user-authored idea text by trusting
    the user-authored line (observed with deepseek-v4-pro through OpenRouter),
    all 30 of its terminal/write_file/patch tool calls landed in the wrong
    repo. The audit caught the resulting empty diff but only after the fact.
    
    Add megaplan/sandbox.py with install_sandbox(project_dir): a context
    manager that pins TERMINAL_CWD to the worktree (mirroring the pattern
    the Claude/CLI happy path already follows at agent/cli.py:7260) and
    wraps the registered handlers for terminal / write_file / patch to
    validate paths before dispatch. Refusal is a hard JSON error returned
    to the model, not silent rewriting. read_file is intentionally not
    sandboxed — the model legitimately needs to read /tmp/<idea>.txt and
    megaplan template files. hermes_worker installs the sandbox whenever
    a phase has toolsets active.
    
    Also escalate scope-drift severity when files_missing is non-empty.
    A claim-without-write (model fabricated work, or work landed elsewhere)
    is at least as bad as a write-without-claim, so it should escalate too.
    
    Tests cover: leading `cd /escape && ...` refusal, leading `cd <project>`
    strip, leading `cd <project>/sub` rewrite to relative, absolute write
    outside project_dir refused, traversal refused, V4A patch directives
    checked, read_file pass-through, env+handler restoration, and an
    end-to-end check that hermes_worker.run_hermes_step actually installs
    the sandbox during agent invocation. All mocked — no models invoked.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

 megaplan/sandbox.py       | 315 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 tests/test_sandbox.py     | 514 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 3 files changed, 841 insertions(+)

diff --git a/megaplan/hermes_worker.py b/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
index c018ef3b..1be386e2 100644
--- a/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
+++ b/megaplan/hermes_worker.py
@@ -565,6 +565,17 @@ def run_hermes_step(
     # The JSON template in the prompt is sufficient; _parse_json_response
     # handles code fences and markdown wrapping.
 
+    # Install the project_dir sandbox whenever a toolset is active.  This
+    # pins TERMINAL_CWD and wraps the terminal/write_file/patch handlers so
+    # the model can't escape the worktree even if its prompt context tells
+    # it to (see megaplan/sandbox.py).  Phases without tools (no toolsets)
+    # don't need it.
+    from contextlib import ExitStack
+    _sandbox_stack = ExitStack()
+    if toolsets:
+        from megaplan.sandbox import install_sandbox
+        _sandbox_stack.enter_context(install_sandbox(project_dir))
+
     # Run — with fallback to OpenRouter for MiniMax if primary API fails
     started = time.monotonic()
     try:
@@ -635,6 +646,7 @@ def run_hermes_step(
                 ) from exc
     finally:
         sys.stdout = real_stdout
+        _sandbox_stack.close()
     elapsed_ms = int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000)
 
     cost_usd = result.get("estimated_cost_usd", 0.0) or 0.0
